**`STAGE 10 — T2-C′ DIFFERENTIAL PRODUCT-VALUE ASSESSMENT: COMPLETED ✅ — DISPOSITION B.`**
Read the next line before drawing any conclusion from that tick.

**`T2-C′ PRODUCT-VALUE CONCLUSION REMAINS PARTIAL. IT HAS NOT PASSED AND IS NOT FULLY
CLOSED.`** Stage 10 is a *differential* task: it asks what materially changed since the
accepted WS16 evidence and whether that alters the accepted conclusion. The Owner-accepted
answer is that **the product materially changed while the verdict stays `PARTIAL`**.
`T2-C′ PASS: NO` · `T2-C′ FULLY CLOSED: NO` · `REAL USER VALUE: UNEVIDENCED` ·
`PRODUCT DIFFERENTIATION: UNEVIDENCED` · `MECHANICAL DEPTH EQUIVALENCE: NOT ESTABLISHED`.

**Controlling baseline, preserved not rewritten.** WS16 is the original
**electronics/electrical-only** validation baseline — `MECHANICAL WS16 BASELINE: NONE /
ZERO COVERAGE`. PDVG-01 (PR #559) is the accepted first differential and carries the
controlling conclusion **`PARTIAL`, not `ADEQUATE`**.

**Electronics differential — `MATERIALLY UPDATED / VERDICT PARTIAL`:** durable saved-project
journey and recovery, bilingual/Arabic serving, correction and withdraw-and-replace, T2-G
questioning, per-question explainability, semantic quality ordering and evidence references,
quantified requirements, contextual feedback, rendered candidate representation,
project-record longitudinal visibility, commercial evidence capture, manufacturing evidence
capture, the Readiness Snapshot, and the Stage-8 relevance repair with its assessment
disclosure. **None of these becomes `REAL USER VALUE PROVEN` or `PRODUCT DIFFERENTIATION
PROVEN`.**

**Mechanical differential — `NEW POST-WS16 DOMAIN / DEPTH EQUIVALENCE NOT ESTABLISHED`.**
Mechanical is an activated product domain with its own governed domain and content surfaces,
so the product materially differs from a baseline that had none; **equivalent depth is not
claimed**. The smaller substance-signal set, the smaller `PHYSICAL_FEASIBILITY` question set,
the electronics-sourced Stage-8 PF derivation and the intentional capability-declaration
exclusions all carry `UNCERTAIN PRODUCT-VALUE SIGNIFICANCE` — **neither defect proven nor
parity proven**, and none authorizes implementation.

**`CANDIDATE REPRESENTATION: PRESENT` · `PLATFORM-SIDE CANDIDATE COMPARISON: ABSENT`.** G-3
changed representation after the `RUN-002` RC and created no comparison engine, so the
historical `RUN-002` result stands as valid historical evidence. **No inference is drawn that
criteria 5 or 6 now pass, or that `T1-A′` is closer to closure.** `T1-A′: OPEN` ·
`FRB: REMAINS` · `CLOSURE EVIDENCE: NOT MET` · `RUN-004: NOT AUTHORIZED`.

**FEATURE EXISTS ≠ EVIDENCE EXISTS ≠ VALIDATED CONCLUSION EXISTS.** Readiness Snapshot
`PARTIAL` material, ceiling `INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE`; commercial capture exists with no
validated commercial conclusion; manufacturing capture exists with no manufacturability
conclusion. No readiness, commercial-readiness or manufacturing-readiness `PASS`.

**MCP: `DEFERRED RECOMMENDATION ONLY` · `TRIGGER: NOT FOUND` · `REQUIRED FOR T2-C′
DISPOSITION: NO` · `IMPLEMENTATION AUTHORIZED: NO`.** No MCP row, no implementation, no
provider selected.

<!-- CURRENT-BLOCK: stage-18-semantic-normalization -->
### Carried into Stage 18 — Multilingual Semantic Normalization Layer

**PRESERVED FOR STAGE-18 ADJUDICATION · NOT AUTHORIZED · NOT IMPLEMENTED as implementation · ONLY A LOCAL-ONLY SHADOW FOUNDATION (PR #693) AND A SYNTHETIC EVALUATION PACK (PR #694) DELIVERED; EXTERNAL / PROVIDER / DURABLE MSNL NOT AUTHORIZED.** Recorded here in
ROUTING so it cannot be lost between stages: **at the BEGINNING of Stage 18 / D13 / CAP-01
adjudication, explicitly reconsider it.** Writing it down starts nothing.

Intended bounded flow: **user input (Arabic / English / future supported language) → semantic
normalization → canonical InventorAI concepts → the EXISTING deterministic progression /
decision engine.** The existing engine remains the decision owner; this layer would sit in
front of it, never inside it.

Safeguards to preserve at Stage-18 adjudication — **the COMPLETE preserved safeguard set,
none of it optional prose**: **shadow-first operation · pinned model / version · confidence
boundary · fail-closed behaviour · deterministic fallback · provider-neutral architecture ·
no automatic concept creation · the model is NEVER the final decision owner · no automatic
readiness promotion · no unsupported engineering conclusion · AUDITABILITY / PROVENANCE ·
PRIVACY / DATA BOUNDARY.** The last two are material safeguards and are written out in full
below, because a bare two-word entry would not carry them.

**AUDITABILITY / PROVENANCE.** Where privacy permits, preserve enough attributable
information to understand: **source input → normalization proposal → selected canonical
concept.** A normalization that cannot be traced back to the input that produced it and the
proposal that was chosen cannot be adjudicated, corrected or withdrawn later.

**PRIVACY / DATA BOUNDARY.** Before ANY external model/provider integration, Stage-18
adjudication must determine **what project/user data may be transmitted outside InventorAI,
and under what privacy, security and retention boundary.** This is preservation for that
later Stage-18 READ-ONLY adjudication only: **no provider is integrated, no provider is
selected, and no live privacy policy is defined here.**

**MSNL STEP 1 — READ-ONLY ADJUDICATION (2026-09-24), THEN A LOCAL-ONLY SHADOW FOUNDATION
(PR #693) AND EVALUATION PACK V1 (PR #694); EXTERNAL / PROVIDER / DURABLE MSNL NOT
AUTHORIZED.** The Owner opened the read-only architecture / data-flow adjudication of this
item; that authorized no runtime, no provider, no model call and no data transmission, and
the preservation statements here still hold. The later shadow foundation's capture is OFF by
default and its sink discards — no provider, no persistence, no inference authority — and the
evaluation pack is synthetic only. The adjudication — and any later step —
must honour: **(1) shadow / proposal first** — before provenance hardening, MSNL output may
only propose a normalization and never becomes authoritative project truth; **(2) closed
concept vocabulary** — map natural-language input only to existing governed canonical
concepts, never inventing one; **(3) precision first** — a false semantic attribution is
more dangerous than an abstention; **(4) abstain is safe** — low confidence or ambiguity
returns ABSTAIN / NO-MAPPING, never a classification forced to raise coverage; **(5) fail
closed with deterministic fallback** — when MSNL abstains or fails, the existing
deterministic engine stays functional and authoritative; **(6) provider neutrality** — no
binding to one vendor or model; **(7) no decision authority** — MSNL never decides gap
status, maturity, readiness, validation, evidence truth, specialist completion or commercial
readiness; **(8) traceability** — where privacy permits, keep source input → normalization
proposal → candidate canonical concept → disposition; **(9) privacy / data minimization** —
before ANY external provider integration, determine what invention / project / user data
may leave InventorAI, the minimum needed, retention, security, provider handling and the
applicable privacy boundary, and never send whole-project context merely because it is
technically convenient.
**STAGE-18 CLOSURE RECONCILIATION (2026-10-01) — `MSNL: FUTURE / DEFERRED / NOT ACTIVATED`.** The Stage-18 reconsideration this note asked for took place (MSNL Step 1 above). The Stage 18 closure — Gap-Scoped Technical Next-Step Guidance — uses no MSNL, LLM or provider, and MSNL was NOT a Stage-18 completion blocker. MSNL stays preserved here as a FUTURE / DEFERRED direction under the existing language-direction NEXT TRIGGER in CLAUDE.md, with every safeguard above intact; Stage 18 completion neither implements it nor attaches it to a new Stage, and any activation still needs its own Owner authorization and data boundary.
<!-- END CURRENT-BLOCK: stage-18-semantic-normalization -->

<!-- CURRENT-BLOCK: d3-fk-hardening -->
### Carried forward — D3 FK HARDENING NOTE (`supporting_evidence_id`)

**CURRENT STATUS: integrity is enforced by the canonical Commercial Evidence owner/store
path** — existence, same project, same dimension, non-self, separation from the supersession
edge, and acyclicity, applied identically on fresh and migrated databases, with the complete
resulting history validated before the durable insert. Enforcement is relocated, not absent.

**NO FRESH-ONLY DATABASE FK IS TO BE ADDED.** Re-evaluate a true composite database FK —
`(project_id, supporting_evidence_id)` → `readiness_evidence(project_id, evidence_id)` —
**only when EITHER** a future migration can safely rebuild `readiness_evidence` for **ALL**
existing databases, so fresh and migrated databases receive identical constraints, **OR** the
datastore moves to a platform such as PostgreSQL where the composite FK can be introduced
consistently and safely.

**At that time:** preserve all existing rows; preserve D1 lifecycle semantics; preserve D2
quantitative semantics; preserve D3 linkage semantics; verify legacy migration compatibility;
ensure fresh and migrated databases enforce the SAME database constraints; and retain
owner/store validation as defence-in-depth even after FK adoption.

**Until then:** no fresh-only FK; no unequal integrity behaviour; and **no SQLite trigger
added merely to imitate part of the constraint** — a partial imitation of a constraint is
harder to reason about than the explicit owner-side rule it would shadow.

**POSSIBLE FUTURE DEFENSE-IN-DEPTH: automated integrity audit** — a periodic or on-demand
sweep confirming that every stored `supporting_evidence_id` still resolves inside its own
project and dimension. **DO NOT BUILD IT NOW SOLELY BECAUSE IT IS POSSIBLE.** This records a
possibility, **not a new implementation mandate**: `NOT IMPLEMENTED` · `NOT AUTHORIZED`. It
neither weakens nor replaces the owner/store enforcement that is current, and it opens no
workstream.
<!-- END CURRENT-BLOCK: d3-fk-hardening -->

## 1. Governing interpretation

This roadmap is reconciled against recorded Git evidence and later superseding records. Historical phase plans and closed-workstream rows remain evidence, not automatic instructions to rebuild absent future capabilities.

**Owner operating direction:** use the minimum necessary governance proportionate to the specific risk. Do not create a document, review, or gate unless it addresses a material risk not covered by existing controls. Prioritize product progress; do not reopen completed work without new evidence of a material defect. The update-at-each-step instruction is incorporated into the existing successor operating rule in §13, not a separate approval workflow.

Status meanings:

- **COMPLETED ✅:** accepted scope is implemented or otherwise formally closed. Do not reopen without new material evidence.
- **BUILT BUT DORMANT 🧊:** source exists but has no authorized/live runtime consumer or activation.
- **RECORDED / NOT AUTHORIZED ⏳:** future capability is preserved, not selected for implementation.
- **DEFERRED — RETURN TRIGGER ⏳:** intentionally postponed until the stated condition.
- **BLOCKED / HOLD ⛔:** work must not proceed until its named decision/evidence dependency is met.
- **IN PROGRESS 🔄:** current authorized lifecycle only.

No checkbox, eligibility statement, recommendation, or roadmap position grants permission to edit, commit, push, merge, deploy, activate a domain/provider, or conduct human research.

   - **Consolidated amendment 2 — event/retry/choice history:** one signed, bounded render token binds exact context, verified owner, browser session, expected current feedback head (or empty), fresh nonce and expiry. Keep one POST, no two-step confirmation or proposal-staging subsystem. Durable event key identifies the rendered submission independently of choice; exact same event/content replays once, same key/different content conflicts. A different rendered event requires the expected head to match within the transaction. A→B→A with fresh forms must produce three legitimate states, while delayed retries cannot restore a superseded selection. Distinguish ALREADY_CURRENT from an exact event replay; no unnecessary row for resubmitting the current choice. Historical replay acknowledgment must not claim that the old choice is still current. Persist-before-acknowledge; resolve uncertain writes by durable event-key readback, never assume success or absence.
   - **Consolidated amendment 3 — executable chain integrity:** one root per (project, context) via a partial unique index on rows with no predecessor; one successor per row via a partial unique index; derive the active head instead of inventing an is-active update. Use a same-project/same-context composite self-FK with a matching UNIQUE parent key, plus project FK, event/sequence uniqueness, bounded choice checks and pre-insert/load validation for same-context, earlier predecessor, no cycle/fork and intact history. No FK to `records`, no alteration of existing tables/rows. The new table is an additive schema migration; do not describe it as “no schema change”. Test fresh/populated-file databases and idempotent reopen. A proposed 1,000-row project cap counts supersessions; refuse a new write clearly at the cap without truncating history or blocking the invention journey; exact replays remain read-only.
   - **Consolidated amendment 4 — cold readback and failure isolation:** cold sessions remain read-only (`domain is None`); use the existing reconstructed state for exact-context readback only, and require existing explicit resume before accepting feedback. Never rehydrate the session from the feedback route. An identity that cannot be reproduced after restart must not inherit a different ask’s feedback or receive a false same-instance claim. An empty history, failed read and corrupt populated history are distinct; suppress/disable only feedback with truthful copy, not the answer journey. Show a selected choice only after verified durable readback. No “sent/reviewed by the team”, anonymity, retention/deletion or learning promise not implemented. Only synthetic test data now; operational retention/erasure and authorized downstream review remain later obligations, not silently solved by capture.

These are not a single bundle. Activate only the capability whose prerequisites and user need are proven.

Stage-18 closure reconciliation (2026-10-01): those prerequisites applied to research-backed knowledge expansion; the Stage 18 closure used only existing governed pack truth (no new D13 research, external source or specialist appointment), and any future research-backed knowledge expansion stays separately gated by them — Research Gate 3 is not reopened.

Stage-19 closure reconciliation (2026-10-01): for the current planning-only scope the failure side is the existing generated `failure_or_revision_condition` (no separate Failure Criterion field), risks stay with the existing risk / safety owners (no per-experiment risk field or new risk / failure model), variables are the inventor's own free-text Test Variable / Condition (no formal experimental variable model), and recorded executions are disclosed only as the inventor's own unvalidated count — never as a result outcome, pass / fail judgement, validation or feasibility.

21. **CAP-10 Contradiction Detector** — deterministic and report-only; never choose a winner.

23. **CAP-06 Multi-Axis Readiness Dashboard** — presentation after readiness axes exist; no hidden weighting or single readiness score.

25. **CAP-13 Thickness/Specification/Safety Advice** — separate from CAP-12; requires calculations/evidence and specialist boundaries. **Navigation pointer (2026-10-04):** the calculation / units ownership boundary on which CAP-13 numerical output depends is the accepted boundary contract of record [`SHARED_DETERMINISTIC_CALCULATION_AND_UNITS_OWNER_BOUNDARY_CONTRACT.md`](SHARED_DETERMINISTIC_CALCULATION_AND_UNITS_OWNER_BOUNDARY_CONTRACT.md) — documentation only; it implements no owner, authorizes no first increment and assigns no CAP number. **Stage 25 entry (2026-10-08):** the delivered Stage 25 — CAP-13 Two-Support Static Reactions — Slice 1 implements that owner's first A2 increment (unnumbered) for ONE method and ONE consumer; Stage 25 is ENTERED / PARTIAL for that slice only and full CAP-13 stays NOT AUTHORIZED.

#### Future Technology Subitems 28-T1…28-T5 — Stage 28 subitems; future bounded technical-responsibility assessments only

**Recorded 2026-10-04 (Owner-authorized documentation sync; no product change).** Status of every item: `RECORDED — PLANNING / NAVIGATION ONLY — NOT AUTHORIZED`. They are NOT assumed new domains, NOT activated, NOT implementation authorizations and NOT new Stages: five subitems of this same Stage 28, like the Satellite subitem below; the top-level count stays 45, and the 28-T subitems add no top-level stage and do not change the incomplete count. *(Superseded 2026-10-04 at the natural touch of the shared calculation / units acceptance record, preserved — was: "the top-level count stays 45 and the incomplete count 24 / 45".)* They do not change Stage 28's current bounded completion (`STAGE 28: COMPLETE — CURRENT BOUNDED CONTROL-LOOP OPTIONAL-PART SCOPE ONLY`): they belong to the NOT-globally-discharged future Stage-28 portfolio, sit outside that bounded completion, are not implemented by the current `control_loop` optional part, do not reopen, invalidate or globally complete Stage 28, start no root activation, and any applicable future Stage-30 reassessment stays proportional to the actual future change. Row 28 stays ticked for its bounded scope only; its tick is not completion of 28-T1…28-T5.

**Pre-implementation gate — every item, separately, before any implementation:** (1) duplication / overlap against existing product owners; (2) extension vs composition vs bounded reasoning layer vs Domain Pack vs defer; (3) architecture trigger; (4) source maturity / technical authority; (5) Source / IP boundary (no source research is performed by this recording; publicly viewable ≠ open license; no vendor content ingestion without source-use review); (6) smallest useful product slice; (7) explicit Owner authorization. No item is authorized merely because it is documented here. Each begins with a Lead-controlled READ-ONLY reassessment under §8C and checklist rule 23.

**28-T1 — Sensors, Instrumentation & Data Acquisition.** Begin with a bounded instrumentation responsibility associated with the existing Electrical / Electronics context. Candidate concerns: sensing requirements, measurement range, resolution, accuracy, sampling, calibration, signal conditioning / acquisition context, ADC / DAQ and uncertainty. CAP-09 remains measurement / validation planning, not instrument-design ownership. No new root domain is created automatically; a standalone owner is justified only when repeated concrete cases demonstrate technical truth that cannot safely remain with existing owners. Current owner overlap must be rechecked at implementation time; sensor physics and circuit-level conditioning must not be silently taken from Electrical / Electronics. **Bounded reference group (28-T1-SENSING-VALUE-THRESHOLD-01, DELIVERED — PR #789; effective only on its verified merge):** ONE prose-only Electronics MECHANISM_COMPLETENESS reference group — a sensed value versus an above-threshold indication — from electronics_electrical:PR012–PR013 (CC BY 4.0, attributed); explanation only, no new owner; every other 28-T1 concern (range, resolution, accuracy, sampling, calibration, conditioning, ADC / DAQ, uncertainty) stays RECORDED — NOT AUTHORIZED.

**28-T2 — Embedded Systems & Firmware.** A bounded embedded-execution responsibility associated with electronic subsystems. Candidate concerns: MCU / SBC / controller boundaries, execution states, timing, interrupts, ADC / PWM, UART / SPI / I²C / CAN, watchdogs, startup / shutdown, fault handling, memory / power modes and bare-metal vs RTOS distinctions. It must NOT become generic Software ownership, an IoT device-management platform, an OTA platform, a fleet-management platform or an autonomous code-generation authority (Capability Register §1A legacy-pattern exclusion 5 is unchanged).

**28-T3 — Power Electronics & Motion Control.** Do NOT assume one broad new Motion domain. Split authority is preserved: Electrical / Electronics owns electrical drive / switching / conversion truth where qualified; Mechanical owns loads, transmission and mechanical suitability where qualified; their interaction is composed through interfaces (Stage 15 remains the interface home); Embedded (28-T2) may later supply execution constraints where applicable; `control_loop` remains within its current bounded concept scope. Candidate concerns: drives, switching, power conversion, PWM, current sensing, protection boundaries, motor / actuator class concepts, power-budget concepts and BLDC / stepper / servo contexts. No final motor, drive, supply, rating or safety sizing is authorized by recording this direction; CAP-13 remains separate.

**28-T4 — PLC / Industrial Automation & Control.** A bounded vendor-neutral automation / sequencing responsibility over the conceptual chain `sensors → inputs → control logic → states / sequences → interlocks → actuators → outputs`. Vendor-neutral core first; the current `control_loop` is NOT sufficient by itself to own PLC / industrial automation; Siemens, Rockwell / Allen-Bradley, Schneider / Modicon, Mitsubishi, Omron, Beckhoff and other vendors are examples only — none is selected, none is the core technical authority, and any future vendor-specific reference layer requires separate source qualification and source / IP review. HMI, SCADA and industrial communications are reassessed separately. No current authority is implied for safety-PLC certification, SIL / PL determination, final I/O engineering, production ladder-code generation, HMI / SCADA implementation or vendor selection.

**28-T5 — Mechatronics / Robotics — Shared Composition Reuse & Residual-Capability Reassessment.** The existing Robotics / Mechatronics direction in row 28 and the Lead continuity rules, now named and sequenced; NOT a new Mechatronics program and NOT a new Robotics program. Default architecture — MECHATRONICS: composition / integration over the bounded technical owners that legitimately participate; it normally owns NO independent technical truth merely because several disciplines participate, its normal role is composition / integration coordination (required contributions, references, interfaces, incompatible assumptions, unresolved cross-domain information), and it must NOT infer compatibility merely because no conflict was detected. ROBOTICS: the SAME composition foundation plus only separately justified robotics-specific residual capabilities, reusing the same design-time composition machinery. Potential residuals are reassessed one by one and MAY include, only when genuinely unowned: localization / state estimation; perception; path / motion planning; bounded robot-specific coordination; other demonstrated residual technical reasoning — never assumed to belong to one Robotics owner. Preference order: existing-owner extension → bounded specialist capability → broader governed owner / domain only with stronger demonstrated evidence. Kinematics is first checked against Mechanical ownership; motion is first decomposed into Mechanical geometry / load, Electrical drive, interfaces, and bounded residual reasoning only if something remains genuinely unowned. A future dedicated Mechatronics or Robotics owner requires ALL of: (1) a concrete technical claim required by a representative project; (2) ownership-gap proof against current and planned owners; (3) proof of a technical-method gap, not merely missing inputs or coordination; (4) bounded authority / output / assumptions / limitations; (5) qualified source authority and a compatible source-use basis; (6) proof that it integrates into the shared composition without duplicate truth. Never a sufficient trigger: the invention contains several technologies; the user wrote "robot"; classification produced a tie. Any future perception residual belongs to the shared Perception / Edge Inference assessment below, never to a second Robotics-owned perception authority.

**Portfolio sequencing — no universal serial dependency.** The window after Stage 27 (THERM-01) is an important planning window for future technical-depth work, not a chain `27 → T1 → T2 → T3 → T4 → T5 → 31`. 28-T1…28-T4 are future portfolio assessments whose actual order is re-evaluated by product value, dependencies, technical authority, overlap and source maturity; 28-T5 occurs when representative integration evidence makes the Mechatronics / Robotics reassessment meaningful. The Technical Realization core proof (§8C C; Owner working label "T6", a Technical Realization direction, NOT a Stage-28 subitem) does NOT require 28-T1…28-T5 to be implemented first. Stage 31 (IoT architecture) requires only the capabilities its bounded use case actually depends on, remains NOT AUTHORIZED, is not entered by this recording, and must not absorb all Embedded, all Sensors, all connectivity-related realization or all Robotics communication; the existing `iot_electronics` pack is legacy-shaped and must be re-qualified before any future participation — its presence is neither IoT implementation nor activation. No artificial serial blocker is created.

#### Named future technical-responsibility assessments — Wireless / RF Connectivity · Battery / Electrochemical Energy Storage / BMS · Perception / Edge Inference (Stage 28 future portfolio; no identifier numbers)

**Recorded 2026-10-05 (Owner-authorized documentation-only recording; no product change).** Architecture reviewed independently by Astra (SAFE AS EXTENSIONS / BOUNDED OWNERS) and by an independent Claude architecture review (SAFE WITH CORRECTIONS); the Lead reconciled both against the live architecture and adopted the shared conclusion plus the stricter ownership correction. Three PRIMARY named assessments join the 28-T1…28-T5 planning portfolio above under stable descriptive names only: they carry NO `28-T` number ("T6" is already the Technical Realization working label and `28-T6` does not exist), and 28-T1…28-T5 keep their numbers and text unchanged. Status of each: `RECORDED — PLANNING / NAVIGATION ONLY — NOT AUTHORIZED`. Each is NOT a new top-level Stage, NOT a Domain Pack, NOT a domain or root domain (root-domain status: NONE / NOT AUTHORIZED), NOT automatically a physical subsystem part and NOT an implementation authorization; none is implemented, qualified, activated, part-eligible or root-admissible. They do not reopen or globally complete Stage 28 (it stays COMPLETE only for its current bounded control-loop optional-part scope), and the top-level and remaining counts are unchanged. The seven-point pre-implementation gate above applies to each, separately.

**Wireless / RF Connectivity** — `RECORDED — PLANNING / NAVIGATION ONLY — NOT AUTHORIZED`. Named "Wireless / RF Connectivity", not generic "Communications", which is too broad and would overlap Embedded (28-T2) and PLC / Industrial Automation (28-T4). Potential bounded responsibility: wireless-link concepts; propagation / channel assumptions; antenna / RF behaviour; range / interference / coexistence concepts; bandwidth / latency concepts; protocol-family constraints; the RF-reception aspects of GNSS where applicable. Must NOT absorb: generic circuit / component / electrical truth (Electrical / Electronics); wired serial buses or firmware execution (28-T2); industrial fieldbus (28-T4); IoT device identity / telemetry / cloud / fleet truth; GNSS positioning / navigation accuracy; EMC / FCC / spectrum / SAR / certification conclusions; arbitrary link-budget numerical execution. IoT (Stage 31) may CONSUME future Wireless / RF truth but must not own or absorb it.

**Battery / Electrochemical Energy Storage / BMS** — `RECORDED — PLANNING / NAVIGATION ONLY — NOT AUTHORIZED`. Named "Battery / Electrochemical Energy Storage / BMS", not unrestricted "Energy Storage". EXTENSION-FIRST is mandatory: the existing Electrical / Electronics owner and the Power Electronics direction (28-T3) are reused first, and if the first real need is only a simple bounded reference fundamental already truthfully supportable by Electrical / Electronics, that existing owner is extended instead of creating a new owner. A separate bounded responsibility becomes justified only when a real product case proves an unowned battery / BMS-specific responsibility, and then only for: electrochemical-storage concepts; chemistry-family operating boundaries; capacity / runtime relationship concepts; charge / discharge envelopes; cycle-life / degradation concepts; BMS functions; cell balancing; battery-specific protection constraints. Must NOT absorb: generic electrical fundamentals; existing power-budget truth; converter / charger power-stage design (28-T3); Embedded BMS execution / firmware (28-T2); THERM-01 thermal analysis; Renewable generation / grid truth; sizing, rating, chemistry selection or safety conclusions without separately authorized methods and evidence.

**Perception / Edge Inference** — `RECORDED — PLANNING / NAVIGATION ONLY — NOT AUTHORIZED`. Named "Perception / Edge Inference", not only "Computer Vision": vision is a possible FIRST MODALITY, not the whole owner, so a future radar, thermal or acoustic perception need does not create a duplicate perception owner. Potential bounded responsibility: perception-task framing; sensing → preprocessing → inference → output reasoning; detection / classification / inspection concepts; perception-specific model / dataset provenance; edge-inference trade-offs; perception uncertainty / limitations; perception-specific evidence framing; bounded sensor fusion only where it supports a qualified perception claim. Must NOT absorb: sensor acquisition / calibration / measurement truth (28-T1); Embedded execution / scheduling / memory / runtime truth (28-T2); Robotics planning / motion / control; generic Software architecture; generic AI / ML ownership; MLOps / training-platform ownership; InventorAI's own AI advisor, WS17, CAP-15 or CAP-17; automatic accuracy, safety or validation conclusions. A perception residual found by the 28-T5 Robotics reassessment belongs here, never to a second Robotics-owned perception authority.

**Core invariant — `TECHNICAL RESPONSIBILITY ≠ PHYSICAL COMPONENT ≠ ROOT DOMAIN`.** The three meanings stay separate. ONE physical project component may receive MANY attributed technical contributions — one radio-containing device: Electrical / Electronics, Wireless / RF, Embedded and IoT contributions; one battery-containing device: Electrical, Battery / BMS, Thermal and Embedded contributions — without duplicate physical components or duplicate domain-specific BOM records. A technical responsibility is never represented as a fictitious physical subsystem part merely to fit the current optional-part slot. The existing Technical Realization principle stays authoritative (`TECHNICAL_REALIZATION_ANCHOR_COMPANION.md` §9.7): ONE physical component → MANY attributed technical contributions → ONE coherent derived realization / engineering package.

**Composition.** The current architecture direction is sufficient: no generic relationship graph, universal engineering framework, arbitrary N-domain framework or generic technical-owner engine. The current runtime composition stays bounded (the required Mechanical + Electrical / Electronics pair plus the one optional `control_loop` part) and is NOT described as supporting arbitrary future owners; any future runtime admission of one of these responsibilities needs its own bounded design and authorization.

**Calculation / method boundary.** The technical owner holds applicability, assumptions, the qualified engineering method and interpretation; a shared deterministic calculation / units owner, when separately authorized, holds numerical execution and units. No RF, Battery or Perception calculator framework is created, and no concept owner invents values, sizing, ratings or applicability merely because a calculation can be executed.

**External engineering tools.** The existing §8B adapter direction is sufficient and unchanged: technical owner → attributed structured engineering intent → external adapter → returned artifact / result → evidence admission → claim assessment / validation, with `TOOL RESULT RECEIVED ≠ EVIDENCE ADMITTED ≠ CLAIM ASSESSED / VALIDATED`. Provider-specific logic stays outside technical-domain truth. Possible future tools include CAD / PCB / EDA / CAE / PLC and similar specialist engineering systems; no provider is selected and no tool integration is authorized.

**Future Stage-30 reassessment.** Before any material future activation of one of these responsibilities, ONE proportional Stage-30 reassessment for the actual change is required; it is not performed now, and no current Stage-30 completion is a universal waiver. Candidate-specific triggers preserved for that future gate — Wireless / RF: classification overlap; bilingual RF terminology; interference / operating-environment assumptions; regulatory / RF-exposure boundary; evidence provenance; standards / source / IP boundary. Battery / BMS: safety; thermal interaction; capacity / runtime evidence; BMS / protection ownership; SafetySignal input-scope decision; hazardous-condition treatment. Perception: privacy / data-source boundary; model / dataset / configuration identity; false-positive / false-negative / abstention semantics; evidence treatment for performance claims; downstream actuation / safety consequences; provider boundary.

**Cross-cutting Technical Deepening source / IP invariant — `TECHNICAL DEEPENING SOURCE RULE: OPEN / LAWFULLY REUSABLE SOURCES ONLY` (Owner-reconfirmed standing rule, recorded 2026-10-06).** InventorAI SHOULD deepen technical knowledge, but governed Technical Deepening relies only on sources whose reuse basis is safe and clear. Preferred sources are public-domain / government technical sources where the use basis is clear, openly licensed technical sources, open-access material whose actual reuse terms permit the intended use, and any other source only where an explicit lawful reuse basis is recorded. `PUBLICLY VIEWABLE ≠ OPENLY REUSABLE`: "open" never means merely free to read — the controlling principle is lawful reuse, not website accessibility. Technical authority alone does not authorize copying, ingesting or republishing a source's expression. Proprietary, standards-body and vendor material — for example IEEE, IEC, ISO, Bluetooth SIG or 3GPP specifications and vendor documentation, depending on their actual source-use terms — may be referenced by identifier where appropriate, but is never ingested into governed technical content unless its license or permission clearly authorizes that use. If the lawful reuse basis is unclear: `DEFER / ABSTAIN / UNABLE TO SUPPORT` — the gap is never filled from a questionable source. Every future governed technical claim keeps its source identity, technical authority / provenance, source-use / license basis, claim scope and limitations. The rule applies proportionally across ALL future Technical Deepening — Wireless / RF Connectivity, Battery / Electrochemical Energy Storage / BMS, Perception / Edge Inference, 28-T1 Sensors, 28-T2 Embedded, 28-T3 Power Electronics, 28-T4 PLC / Industrial Automation, 28-T5 Mechatronics / Robotics and any later Technical Deepening — not only Wireless / RF. It restates, for this portfolio, the standing Technical Knowledge & Intellectual Property Source Boundary in `CLAUDE.md`, and it authorizes no source ingestion, provider, retrieval or implementation.

**Secondary future portfolio notes — notes only: no Stage subitem, no owner creation, no implementation authorization.** C4 — Hydraulics / Pneumatics: extension-first from Mechanical; a bounded fluid-power owner only if a real method gap later proves necessary. C5 — Optics / Photonics: DEFER; may justify a bounded specialist owner later; no root-domain commitment. C6 — Advanced Materials: DEFER; too broad for an owner now; concrete needs route first through Mechanical / CAP-12 / CAP-13 or the applicable qualified technical owner. C7 — Acoustics / Vibration: structural vibration is a Mechanical extension first; sensing / acquisition goes to the Sensors owner (28-T1) where applicable; an acoustic-method residual is reassessed only when a concrete use case proves it.

#### Satellite Technology — Stage 28 subitem; future decision orchestration only

**Preserved name:** `SATELLITE TECHNOLOGY DOMAIN / SPACE-SYSTEM DECISION ORCHESTRATION`.
**Status:** `FUTURE PRODUCT DIRECTION — RUNTIME IMPLEMENTATION NOT AUTHORIZED`.
**Provenance:** Owner-supplied `Pasted text(20260912-131459).txt`, 40 sections; the Owner asked for an opinion and an appropriate execution time. This request permits this local planning update, not satellite implementation, provider selection, expert appointment or domain activation. The source's concepts are preserved; first-slice boundaries and placement below are Lead planning recommendations, not claims that those functions already exist.

**Chosen planning window.** Existing **Stage F / Stage 28**, using **Stage 30 before any activation**, after the core journey and the relevant shared technical/evidence/decision/commercial foundations in Stages 12–17 are stable and usable for the selected slice. Preserve the recorded IoT → drone/unmanned → renewable planning priority; place satellite consideration after that existing priority rather than silently displacing it. This is portfolio priority, not a claim that satellites technically require completed IoT, drone or renewable implementations. The stage numbers are navigation, not an instruction to execute unrelated optional work first. Do not invent a calendar delivery date from an unestimated roadmap. Satellite support is an optional later specialist expansion, **not a newly imposed first serious-release, paid-launch or core-journey blocker**. Existing release conditions remain unchanged.

**Immediate work is unchanged.** Finish the active PR #641 lifecycle and subsequent already-planned core work. Do not interrupt that lane, return completed Group 1 work to review, or conduct the full satellite reconciliation now. At the Stage 28 selection point, examine only satellite-relevant dependencies; Stage 30 already owns the cross-domain checks. No new permanent review, gate, contract-only PR, full-history audit or separate roadmap is created by recording this direction.

**Preferred minimum shape.** Evaluate an orchestration/composition layer or smaller space-specific reasoning layer first, rather than assume a large new domain engine. The first useful candidate should focus on **mission/use-case need, existing Earth-observation data/services and ground-segment applications**. Establish the user's problem and buyer/user, intended output, coverage, spatial resolution, revisit frequency, latency, data requirement and material constraints. Compare existing satellite services/data, hosted payloads and terrestrial alternatives against a proposed new spacecraft only when those alternatives are relevant. Return a source-linked provisional need assessment, missing evidence, the relevant specialist task with its inputs/expected result, and **one Primary Next Action**. Do not generate orbit/RF/power/thermal/structural designs in this slice. Avoid live API integrations initially when a source-backed comparison or bounded manual specialist handoff suffices. This is a recommended first slice, not removal of the wider direction.

**Reuse and conditional dependencies.** Reuse the canonical Domain Registry and activation policy, current Mechanical and Electronics/Electrical capabilities, canonical requirements/evidence/gap models and FDC-001 / DecisionRecord where their scope actually permits. CAP-01/D13 and CAP-04 retain technical-guidance/action-package responsibilities; CAP-05/07 retain decision composition responsibilities; CAP-11 and the canonical evidence axes retain evidence meaning. Existing ownership must not be confused with an implemented or activated capability. CAP-18 remains the bounded commercial-snapshot owner, not a full financial or commercial system. Reconcile integrated Manufacturing/Integration/System readiness with its own owners; do not invent a satellite score or parallel DecisionRecord.

CAP-12 and CAP-13 remain distinct and separately bounded. Their completion, THERM-01, CAD/FEA and propulsion tools are **not blanket prerequisites** for a data/service need-assessment slice. They become dependencies only when the proposed slice actually supplies materials, specifications, physical analysis or matching readiness claims. Reuse of a terrestrial engineering capability never proves space qualification. Do not automatically activate Software, Communications, IoT, Drones, Renewable Energy, CAP-12/13, Commercial Readiness, live AI or any deferred capability.

**Complete future scope retained — not a mandatory all-at-once implementation:**

|---|---|

Falsify the assumed need for a large new domain. |

**Evidence-return boundary.** A specialist-tool result does not automatically close a gap, promote evidence or certify mission success. Any future return path must preserve the source, input assumptions, applicable units/scope, tool/model version, limitations and validation basis, then use the canonical authorized acceptance/writer path. Existing API/export foundations do not prove a reverse ingestion path exists. Tool-result ingestion/acceptance is a conditional later integration requirement, not authority to build it now. Confidence concerns evidence/conclusion quality, not a fabricated probability of mission success. The source's `JUSTIFIED`, `PASS_WITH_CONDITIONS` and technical/manufacturing/commercial readiness examples may only be mapped to approved meanings supported by evidence. Absence of evidence is not proof that a new satellite is unnecessary; distinguish “not yet justified” from “ruled out.”

**Future return at the existing Stage 28 domain-selection touch.** Produce one consolidated answer to source §38 A–X: overlap; genuine space-specific residuals; reuse/delegation/owning boundaries; mission/need/payload/communications models; power/ADCS/orbit/thermal/structural boundaries; ground segment; regulatory/security; Manufacturing/CAP-12/CAP-13/Commercial relationships; tool-return integration; decision snapshot; next action; evidence/gaps; product value; complexity; smallest implementation and later expansion; existing responsible owner and applicable authorization. These are questions for a proportionate design return, **not 24 new documents or gates**. Source §36 options A–E remain open: new domain, composition domain, extension, smaller reasoning layer, or deferral where existing capabilities suffice. B/D are the current preference, not a prematurely selected architecture.

**Value and activation test within existing controls.** Select one concrete intended customer/use case and use domain-qualified, inspectable sources and the existing qualification/evaluation process to establish that the slice changes a decision or identifies an actionable evidence gap; a new category/menu and generic advice alone are not the product value sought. Reuse existing evidence where valid; recording this direction authorizes no new human study or customer outreach. Before activation satisfy the relevant Stage 30 content, bilingual/RTL, domain-label, safety, subsystem/evidence and non-interference controls, with the bounded owner/scope and explicit Owner implementation authority already required by the domain program. Do not turn all deferred readiness/capability items into blanket blockers.

**Sequence preserved:** RECONCILE → DEFINE MINIMUM SATELLITE-SPECIFIC SCOPE → IDENTIFY OWNER → IDENTIFY THE EXISTING APPLICABLE GATE → OWNER AUTHORIZATION → IMPLEMENTATION. No satellite architecture, source/provider, new classifier taxonomy or runtime capability is selected by this local entry. ### 8A. MCP strategic direction — recommendation carried, no implementation authority

The Owner requested a read-only strategic-fit opinion after the t2g2 instruction. The outgoing Lead recommended **DEFER IMPLEMENTATION / consider a narrowly scoped read-only MCP server first**, not an MCP client first. The assessment is complete in this conversation; it is NOT the response currently awaited from Claude and must not interrupt t2g2.

Reuse `engine/read_export_service.py` and `web/api_v1.py`: the current machine-principal public API has exactly project read and structured export. Its outward projection is intentionally limited; full deliverable, validation-plan, project-list and next-action exposure is NOT already authorized merely because those derivations exist. Reuse `engine/session_reconstruction.py`, `engine/deliverable_assembler.py`, `engine/validation_plan.py` and `engine/idea_development_outputs.py` without a second business-logic/truth layer. Existing API reads write audit/rate-limit records; “read-only” means no mutation of invention/business state, not an absence of all security writes.

Proposed minimum later scope: one chosen compatible host, one explicitly selected authorized project, a minimized state view and one selected existing output section. Do not freeze illustrative tool names, enable project enumeration by default, assume all hosts have identical support, or expose full invention content automatically. Compare the existing API/export alternative before selecting MCP.

Return at **Stage 10** for demonstrated workflow/user value; any production implementation remains part of the existing connector direction with applicable **Stages 38/39** security/privacy/operational dependencies, not a new numbered gate and not a universal first-release blocker. A focused architecture consultation belongs in the normal design step if remote delegation/auth/data exposure changes materially. This recommendation has NOT become an Owner adoption or an implementation mandate.

Preserve: IP confidentiality; user/project/host/field-scoped authorization and explicit disclosure consent; revocation limits after data has been shared; no reused browser session as machine authority; no shared master credential; CSRF is not machine authorization; prompt-injection/untrusted-content handling; no generic commands or arbitrary URL tools; no write/research/evidence/readiness actions; exact snapshot/version and stale-state limits; privacy-minimal audit/action provenance; bounded rate/cost/output/compatibility; no external result automatically promoted to supplied/verified evidence. The complete Owner read-only request had fourteen risk categories; these categories remain covered here and in the handover, not a fresh security certification. No MCP SDK, connection, pilot, real-user exposure, provider or repository change was performed.

### 8B. External Engineering Tools & Simulation Interoperability — future direction, no implementation authority

**Recorded at v1.32 as a FUTURE PLANNED PRODUCT DIRECTION. Status: `PLANNED / DEFERRED`. NOT a current release blocker. No new Stage is created for it.**

**Where it belongs:** **Stage 36** (CAP-15 provider abstraction + CAP-17 prompt/model configuration) is the primary home, because the required property is provider abstraction. Secondary notes attach at **Stages 18–27** (Technology Deepening, where an external tool would supply technical depth), **Stages 28/31** (domain integration) and **Stages 38/39** (security, privacy and operational dependencies). Phase-7 continues to own export/adapter boundaries.

**Direction.** InventorAI should NOT attempt to build every engineering simulator, solver or specialist engineering tool internally. A future **provider-neutral External Engineering Tools & Simulation Interoperability Layer** may instead allow optional integration with suitable external engineering, simulation, analysis, validation and specialist technical tools and services. This is consistent with the existing Capability Enrichment Register §1A, whose recorded "not recommended" legacy patterns already begin with physics/multi-physics simulation engines and broad domain engineering simulation suites.

**Provider neutrality is binding.** **NO external provider or platform is selected, mandated, preferred, canonical or contracted.** Any platform discussed historically is an example or candidate only. Future selection must be decided on objective fit at that time, considering: capability fit · technical quality · API / CLI / MCP / file interoperability · licensing · privacy and security · reliability · portability · vendor-lock-in risk · user cost · evidence provenance. The architecture must remain provider-neutral and substitutable.

**Preferred future commercial model — OPTIONAL, USER-TRIGGERED, ON-DEMAND PAID EXECUTION THROUGH INVENTORAI:**

- external engineering/simulation use is **optional** and not automatically available to every user;
- the base subscription must **not** automatically absorb external-tool costs;
- the user chooses to invoke an external tool when needed;
- the **price or maximum expected charge is shown BEFORE execution**;
- the user **explicitly confirms** the charge, and only then may the tool be invoked;
- the result returns to InventorAI with clear **provenance**;
- **no external tool becomes canonical truth**;
- **no external simulation automatically promotes readiness to `PASS`**;
- **a provider failure must never silently become a positive technical conclusion**.

**Bring-your-own account / API key: FALLBACK OPTION ONLY**, not the preferred primary commercial model.

**Future implementation would likely require** (considerations only — no current obligation and no new governance gate): a tool registry; a provider-neutral adapter interface; an entitlement/access gate; explicit paid-use consent; a price / maximum-charge gate; usage accounting; idempotency and double-charge protection; provider failure handling; evidence and provenance import through the canonical authorized acceptance path; credential and connection handling; and an interchange/fallback mode where appropriate.

**CAD / PCB boundary preserved.** **Automatic CAD / PCB generation is NOT part of the current product direction.** External-tool interoperability does **not** authorize automatic CAD generation, automatic PCB generation or autonomous manufacturing-file generation. Where future interoperability touches CAD- or PCB-adjacent tools, that is integration and interoperability only unless separately authorized. The dedicated PCB domain remains deferred, and reuse of a terrestrial engineering capability never proves space qualification.

**Nothing is implemented, selected, connected, piloted or authorized by recording this direction.**

### 8C. Cross-stage capability-integration invariants — anti-drift operating rules, NOT a gate

**Derived from existing architecture; they create no Stage, Workstream, tracking ID,
register, authorization gate or approval step, and they grant no implementation
authority.** They exist because the first bounded Stage-18 / CAP-01 increment showed how
easily a first slice hardens into permanent architecture, and how easily a new capability
absorbs an owner that already exists.

- **A. EXISTING OWNER FIRST.** Before creating a truth owner, engine, store, registry or
  model, identify the existing owner and extend or consume it. Do not duplicate ownership
  merely because a new Stage or CAP needs the data.
- **B. PRODUCE ONCE — CONSUME MANY.** Canonical technical/evidence truth is produced once
  by its proper owner and consumed by many capabilities. No per-CAP duplicate evidence,
  risk, assumption, contradiction, readiness, project-identity or domain-taxonomy store.
- **C. CAPABILITY OWNERSHIP DOES NOT COLLAPSE.** A capability may consume another's
  output; it must not silently absorb that capability's responsibility. **CAP-01** bounded
  technical / unresolved-technical-gap guidance · **CAP-04** gap action packages ·
  **CAP-09 / WS-PFV-001** experiment planning and physical validation · **CAP-12**
  prototype materials and manufacturing advisory · **CAP-13** specification, thickness and
  bounded safety advisory · **CAP-14** static visual interpretation and user-confirmed
  component inventory · **THERM-01** future thermal calculation/analysis where separately
  authorized · **CAP-08** assumptions · **CAP-10** contradictions · **CAP-11**
  evidence-quality ladder · **CAP-06** multi-axis readiness PRESENTATION, not readiness
  truth ownership · **CAP-07** decision-room COMPOSITION, not independent truth creation ·
  **Technical Realization** the shared technical-capability layer for future technical
  producers and artifacts, not a duplicate CAP-01 engine. Its recorded future product
  direction — **Technical Realization — Preliminary Component / Prototype Realization &
  Engineering Handoff Direction** (Owner working label "T6": handover / navigation
  shorthand only; NOT a Stage-28 subitem, NOT `28-T6`, NOT a domain, NOT a new Capability
  Register number; recorded 2026-10-04; NOT AUTHORIZED) — is a cross-capability
  realization / composition direction, not a technical authority: it composes attributed
  contributions and references, exposes missing or conflicting engineering information and
  prepares structured engineering intent for a later Stage 36 / §8B tool handoff, and it
  never calculates, selects, sizes, rates, dimensions, validates, certifies, determines
  safety, declares compatibility or invents missing technical truth. Its home is the
  existing Technical Realization architecture (`TECHNICAL_REALIZATION_ANCHOR_COMPANION.md`
  §9; `TECHNICAL_REALIZATION_EVIDENCE_AND_ARTIFACT_MODEL.md` §11), not a new document.
- **D. FIRST IMPLEMENTATION ≠ PERMANENT ARCHITECTURE.** A first domain, first provider,
  first language seam, first datastore or first capability slice must not silently become
  the architecture. Current implementation limitations must stay explicitly
  distinguishable from target architecture.
- **E. DOMAIN-SPECIFIC KNOWLEDGE / DOMAIN-AGNOSTIC CORE.** Domain-specific technical
  knowledge stays outside domain-agnostic core decision/progression logic. **No future
  CAP-01 profile may require domain-name branching in core progression or readiness
  logic.**
- **F. CURRENT SINGLE DOMAIN IS AN ADAPTER LIMITATION.** Today's assembled package exposes
  a single root capability/domain. That is current runtime reality, **not** the permanent
  CAP-01 domain model; future project architecture may carry multiple domains and
  subsystem-level domain context. Present CAP-01 presentation must remain extendable to
  that without replacing the basic presentation/profile mechanism.
- **G. LANGUAGE.** The first CAP-01 profile renders bounded EN/AR copy through the
  existing `ui_lang` / `t()` seam. That is the current rendering adapter and does **not**
  establish UI locale as the permanent generated-output language authority; the separately
  preserved future output-language capability is untouched.
- **H. FULL CAP-01 PRESERVED.** The first slice is presentation-only and class-general.
  CAP-01 as a full future capability is **not** permanently limited to static presentation.
  Its preserved intended future scope — when separately governed and supported — still
  includes the exact unresolved technical subproblem, the relevant technical subdomain, the
  specific missing technical information, precise research direction and search terms,
  required measurements / tests / documents, can/cannot verify, uncertainty and risk,
  specialist category ONLY when evidence-supported, and provenance.
- **I. GUIDANCE OWNER ≠ ALL TECHNICAL PRODUCTION.** CAP-01 may produce bounded technical
  guidance. It must not duplicate separately-owned specialized calculations, materials,
  specifications, visual interpretation, physical validation, thermal analysis or other
  authorized technical-production capabilities.
- **J. CROSS-ROADMAP CHECK BEFORE MATERIAL FUTURE WORK.** For future Technology Deepening,
  Domain Expansion, Readiness, Manufacturing, Integration or AI implementation
  instructions, the instruction author checks materially overlapping existing and future
  owners before implementation. This is part of the existing pre-send / anti-drift
  discipline in §11 — **it is NOT a new approval gate.**

### 8D. Technology Coverage & First-Release Traceability — derived navigation; no authority; no activation

**Recorded 2026-10-09 (Owner-directed documentation-only coverage integration; no product change).** This subsection
consolidates, in ONE place, every engineering technology and cross-cutting engineering capability the repository already
records, so that none can silently disappear before a first release. It is DERIVED NAVIGATION under the same rule as the
rest of this roadmap: it creates no Stage, subitem, tracking ID, register number, owner, gate, approval step or
implementation authority; it changes no Stage identifier, count, checkbox or completion declaration; it activates no
domain, admits no method and expands no shared owner. Each row reuses the existing owner it names (8C-A EXISTING OWNER
FIRST; 8C-B PRODUCE ONCE — CONSUME MANY). Where a row and the owning contract, register entry or Stage row differ, the
owner wins and the difference is a defect of this table. FCORA (Stage 43) remains the audit gate that detects
disappearance; this table is the inventory it can audit, not a second gate.

**First-release decision basis — Owner policy clarification (supplied through the Lead; confirmed 2026-10-10).** The
policy is recorded once in the current-authority section of [`ACTIVE_INCREMENT_CONTRACT.md`](ACTIVE_INCREMENT_CONTRACT.md)
as an Owner policy clarification (not an active increment), with the direct Owner wording distinguished from the Lead's
non-verbatim interpretation.
Applied here: a technology with an **evidenced approval to deliver** must be implemented, deepened, integrated where
relevant and tested before public launch, within the bounded scope that approval names; the latest policy supplies that
pre-launch timing, so an older approval need not contain the words "first release". Approval to study, review
architecture, record a direction or preserve a future option is NOT approval to deliver, and approval is never inferred
from inclusion in this roadmap, the Capability Register, historical discussion or a planning portfolio. Four facts stay
separate on every row: (a) the approved capability and its bounded scope; (b) implementation authorization; (c) the
implementation / qualification state (the state-ladder column); (d) acceptance criteria and acceptance results. Missing
acceptance criteria never cancel an evidenced approval; they are recorded as open. A delivered bounded method is never
promoted into approval of a whole engineering discipline. Each row's release decision takes one evidence-bound value,
with its evidence in the release-decision classification below the table: `R1 — FIRST-RELEASE REQUIRED — EVIDENCED
DELIVERY APPROVAL (BOUNDED SCOPE)` · `R2 — APPROVED STRATEGIC DIRECTION — DELIVERY APPROVAL UNRESOLVED` · `R3 — PROPOSED /
STUDY ONLY` (deferred until explicitly approved) · `R4 — EXPLICITLY DEFERRED OR EXCLUDED` · `R5 — OWNER DECISION EVIDENCE
NOT AVAILABLE`. Where approval or supersession scope is ambiguous the row reads R2, never an assumed approval and never an
assumed post-launch deferral. An R1 value authorizes nothing, activates nothing and proves no readiness; it does not waive
qualification, security, IP, evidence, activation or release controls, and a release value never changes a row's
implementation, qualification, activation or bounded-completion state.
*(Superseded 2026-10-10 by the Owner policy clarification above, preserved — was: "**First-release decision basis — Owner
release policy (received 2026-10-09; authoritative).** … **No row currently qualifies for R1:** no repository record names
a first-release technology set or defines "sufficiently deepened" …")*
*(Superseded 2026-10-09 by the Owner release policy above, preserved — was: "**Initial-release technology set: NOT
RECORDED IN THE REPOSITORY.** … Every row below therefore carries `OWNER RELEASE DECISION RECONCILIATION REQUIRED` … it is
NOT a deferral, NOT a denial and NOT an approval.")*

**State ladder (each state is distinct; a later state never implies an earlier one was skipped, and an earlier one never
implies a later one):** `RECORDED` — named in a governance surface · `QUALIFIED` — source-qualified pack or accepted
method contract with its admission blockers closed · `IMPLEMENTED` — merged code · `ACTIVATED` — live for users at
runtime (domain activation is owned by `engine/domain_activation.py` only) · `TESTED` — a description of identified test
evidence (focused tests and the hosted CI required checks green on the tested PR head / proposed merge), not a separate
lifecycle; post-merge identity and tree checks are a distinct record · `RELEASE-READY` — the Owner-recorded release acceptance for that technology is
met. **`RELEASE-READY` IS NOT ASSERTED FOR ANY ROW.** A document, Domain Pack, reference equation, classification signal or
unit test never makes a technology release-ready. Classification signals in a pack (for example MCU, UART, Bluetooth,
sensor, battery, motor) are classification vocabulary only; they are NOT qualified engineering guidance for the technology
they name.

**Classification legend (exactly one per row, with its evidence):** `A — OWNER-APPROVED, BOUNDED (EVIDENCED)` · `B —
RECORDED STRATEGIC DIRECTION, NOT AUTHORIZED` · `C — DEFERRED` · `D — EXPLICIT EXCLUSION` (the excluded shape only; the
remainder of the row keeps its own class) · the release decision in the last column uses the R1–R5 values defined above, each tied
to the evidence in the release-decision classification below the table. An older planning-only or deferred class never overrides a later explicit Owner
decision; a later decision is recorded by its own surface and this table follows it.

**Standing restrictions this table preserves (none relaxed):** the delivered CAP-13 two-support reactions method stays the
only admitted Mechanical method and the bounded stiffness method stays `NOT ADMITTED` with `D-7: OPEN`
(`METHOD ADMISSION: BLOCKED UNTIL D-7 IS CLOSED`); the shared deterministic calculation / units owner holds exactly two
admitted methods under Correction 02 and admits nothing by this table (a further method needs method / source qualification
and an explicit admission correction to that contract, which is not yet proposed or accepted and is not an artifact-only
change); the subsystem composition admits exactly Mechanical + Electrical / Electronics plus at most ONE optional
`control_loop` part (`validate_composition` in `engine/subsystem_model.py`), so a second optional part is not available by
adding a domain name, and any later expansion must preserve existing two- and three-part compositions, stable part
identities, interface references, loading, withdrawal readability and part-identity question targeting (no N-domain
framework or schema change is approved here); Stage 27 is COMPLETE for its single-path bounded
scope only and `FULL THERM-01: NOT AUTHORIZED`; Mechatronics / Robotics stay composition-first with no automatic
compatibility or safety claim; Stage 28's bounded completion is NOT completion of the future-domain program and Stage 30
stays mandatory before any new-domain activation; no second component database, procurement platform or unlicensed
external catalog ingestion; outbound interchange is distinct from live integration and automatic CAD / PCB generation
stays excluded (§8B); `PUBLICLY VIEWABLE ≠ OPENLY REUSABLE` and no new source material is imported by this recording.

decision surfaces. (2) Whether any such approval supersedes the DOMEX-D1 schedule for IoT, Drone, Renewable or Space.
(3) Acceptance criteria and acceptance results for every R1 row. Promoting an R2 or R3 row, or lifting an R4 exclusion,
needs its own explicit Owner decision; this classification does neither, and it creates no gate, Stage, register or
approval step.

**Engineering-order constraints (architecture review findings).** Stage-number order is not itself an engineering
dependency: implementation order follows actual dependencies, architectural readiness and avoidance of duplicate work.
Representative inventions test coverage and integration; they never replace, narrow or discharge an approved technology's
own acceptance. Reusing an existing owner does not make a missing specialist capability implemented, and architectural
deferrability is not permission to move an approved technology beyond launch.

**Hosting rule used by this table.** An incomplete Stage hosts its items directly. A Stage that is COMPLETE for a bounded
scope hosts an ADDED bounded capability under its existing architecture only where its closure text already says that
future work is separately reassessed and authorized (Stages 18, 27, 28 and 36 say so); that is an addition, never a
reopening of the completed scope. The 19 incomplete Stages that host no engineering technology (11, 13, 14, 32, 33, 34,
37, 38–45) are evidence and release gates, not technology homes. No row needs a new Stage.

**What this table does not do.** It decides no launch inclusion; it admits no method; it activates no domain; it opens
no Stage; it names no external provider; it imports no source material; it overturns no exclusion; it adds no tracking
system. Its rows are reconciled by the Owner through the existing decision surfaces, and its "next increment" cells are
proposals that need their own Owner authorization and, where stated, their own contract, source qualification, Stage 30
reassessment or architecture review.

## 10. Review-loop compression

`OWNER REVIEW-LOOP COMPRESSION REQUIREMENT — STANDING PROCESS RULE`

- `SUCCESSOR PROPAGATION REQUIRED: YES`
- `SINGLE-PASS DEFECT ACCUMULATION REQUIRED: YES`
- `CREATOR PRE-FREEZE TRANSITIVE CONSEQUENCE SWEEP REQUIRED: YES`
- `TRANSITIVE SWEEP IS DEPENDENCY-BOUNDED — NO SCOPE EXPANSION`
- `MINOR / NON-BLOCKING FINDING ALONE MUST NOT CREATE A STANDALONE REPAIR LIFECYCLE`
- `DEFERRED FINDINGS REQUIRE CLASSIFICATION, NON-BLOCKING BASIS, AND RETURN TRIGGER OR LATEST-SAFE TOUCH`
- `EARLY INDEPENDENT REVIEW RULE: ACTIVE`
- `DIFFERENTIAL RE-REVIEW DEFAULT FOR BOUNDED REPAIR: YES`
- `MINIMUM INDEPENDENT INVARIANT RECHECK REQUIRED`
- `REVIEW-INVALIDATING STOP EXCEPTION: ACTIVE`
- `COMPLETE DEFECT SET BEFORE CONSOLIDATED CORRECTION`

**Owner-adopted operating application — 2026-09-13.** The Owner explicitly approved applying the agreed reduction of repeated work without impairing project quality. This refines the existing standing rule above; it creates no document/review/approval gate and does not issue `T2G-VERSIONED-IMPLEMENT-01` or adopt its pending version-policy/ownership choices.

- **Read and verify to the risk:** within the same session, reuse already-read sources and evidence tied to their exact revision; inspect the changed files and their directly affected dependencies. Revalidate live identity at required material-action boundaries, on a new session, or when a change/conflict could invalidate the evidence. Do not re-fetch the repository or reread its history for an explanatory message or unchanged repeated report. Required repository boot/authority checks remain applicable; unverified continuity is not assumed.
- **Keep the slice fixed:** target one clearly stated product result within the issued scope. Add work only for a demonstrated material dependency, and surface the smallest necessary scope decision rather than expanding silently or seeking per-file approvals for work already authorized. Efficiency does not weaken security, state/replay integrity, data preservation, bilingual behavior or truthful user outcomes.
- **Continue by delta:** subsequent instructions carry the changed requirement, exact applicable identity, necessary safeguards and expected return; reference accessible unchanged instructions instead of copying the full contract/history. Supply missing material context when the recipient does not have it. Never resend consumed implementation/review/merge instructions as fresh work.
- **Test where the risk changes:** during development use focused behavioral tests, directly affected tests and minimum invariant checks. Exercise the actual user path where relevant; labels and row counts alone are not proof of correct behavior. Keep all existing required final regression/JUnit/hosted-CI checks on the final candidate. Do not repeat full runs after every small edit or duplicate independent full-suite evidence solely for reassurance. Reuse evidence only while its code, test and environment premises remain applicable; no omitted mandatory check, new skip or weakened assertion to save time.
- **Review once, repair by difference:** accumulate the complete known material finding set in one return; consolidate corrections and use the existing independent differential re-review with the minimum affected-invariant recheck. A non-blocking observation remains classified with its return trigger and does not independently generate another repair lifecycle. New material evidence may justify a bounded expansion, not an automatic historical audit.
- **Report and propagate economically:** lead with the user-visible result, remaining material obstacle and the one Owner decision needed, if any. Keep technical execution details in the executor instruction. Update only affected checkpoint/checklist rows when state changes; unchanged explanatory replies need neither a new artifact version nor a full checklist reprint. Carry these practices within the same existing checklist/handover for successor agents; do not create a parallel tracker or assume an external session received them automatically.

## 11. Execution-instruction pre-send rule

Every load-bearing instruction must pass the existing `OWNER MATERIAL EXECUTION-INSTRUCTION PRE-SEND CHECKLIST`: authority/provenance, exact base, exact scope, forbidden changes, dependency-bounded consequences, no unresolved load-bearing discretion, STOP conditions, evidence return and lifecycle truth. Checklist PASS is not execution or merge authority.

## 12. STOP conditions for every successor

Stop at the smallest read-only resolution if: repository/branch/HEAD/PR identity conflicts; awaited controlling review is missing; authorization is absent or exhausted; a proposed feature duplicates current behavior; a closed workstream is being reopened merely because its future module is absent; a dormant component is activated before dependencies; human data or external sources are used without authority; a domain/provider is activated implicitly; an implementation invents readiness, feasibility, safety, legal, manufacturing or commercial conclusions; or the instruction fails its pre-send control.

## 13. Successor operating rule

Before each material execution transition, verify the live branch and current PR first, read the concise current state plus only the load-bearing owner documents for the selected increment, reconcile this roadmap against later merges, and update statuses rather than duplicating rows. The next agent must never execute the next numbered item merely because it is next; it executes only the first dependency-satisfied item explicitly authorized by the Owner. A source-based status update alone does not require a new repository audit. Apply the Owner-adopted read/delta/test/report practices in §10; scope and authorization remain unchanged.

**Owner-requested end-of-step update (2026-09-12):** after each operational step, update the affected existing checklist row(s), the corresponding execution checkpoint, the supported result/evidence reference, and the exact next action or outstanding authorization. Keep the same stage numbers, completed scope and deferred return triggers. End the user-facing step response with a compact “Master Checklist — end-of-step update” showing what actually changed and what remains; use the five-stage operating group rather than reprinting all 45 stages each time. The full checklist remains in this working document and accompanies a successor handover.

Distinguish PREPARED, OWNER-ISSUED, SENT, EXECUTED, REVIEWED, MERGED and POST-MERGE VERIFIED; never advance a status on intention alone. A proposal is not a sent instruction, a failed review is a completed review but not an accepted candidate, and a passed test is not merged delivery. If nothing material changes, say so rather than inventing a new task. Claim a file update only when the revised artifact was actually written and supplied; a chat note is not a repository-document update.
